Generating structures and sequences 126

Using AI to create new molecules, proteins, and complex structures from scratch by learning patterns from existing data, rather than simulating every atom.  open the full subtheme page →

Designing and predicting proteins

18

Using AI to create new protein sequences and 3D shapes, either by learning from known structures or designing entirely new ones that fold correctly.

ICLR 2026

SimpleFold: Folding Proteins is Simpler than You Think

Problem. Protein folding models typically employ complex domain-specific architectures with multiple training objectives, raising the question of whether these designs are truly necessary.
Approach. We introduce SimpleFold, the first flow-matching based protein folding model using only general-purpose transformer blocks with adaptive layers and a generative objective plus structural term.
Contribution. SimpleFold-3B achieves competitive performance on standard folding benchmarks and demonstrates strong ensemble prediction performance, challenging the reliance on complex domain-specific designs.
flow matchingtransformer architectureprotein foldinggenerative modeling

ICLR 2026

Multi-state Protein Design with DynamicMPNN

Problem. Existing multi-state protein design approaches rely on post-hoc aggregation of single-state predictions, achieving poor experimental success rates compared to single-state design.
Approach. DynamicMPNN is an inverse folding model explicitly trained to generate sequences compatible with multiple conformations through joint learning across conformational ensembles.
Contribution. DynamicMPNN outperforms ProteinMPNN by up to 25% on decoy-normalized RMSD and by 12% on sequence recovery across multi-state protein benchmarks when evaluated using AlphaFold 3.
protein designinverse foldingmulti-state modeling
ICLR 2026

Flow Autoencoders are Effective Protein Tokenizers

Problem. Protein structure tokenizers rely on bespoke invariant components challenging to optimize and scale, limiting multimodal protein models.
Approach. Kanzi is a flow-based tokenizer using diffusion autoencoder with flow matching loss, replacing frame-based representation and SE(3)-invariant attention with standard components.
Contribution. Simplifies tokenization and outperforms existing approaches on reconstruction with fraction of model size/training cost; outperforms similar generative models.
flow matchingprotein tokenizationdiffusion autoencoder
ICLR 2026

One protein is all you need

Problem. Protein language models generalize poorly to unseen proteins; pre-training on all proteins limits capacity to excel on specific ones practitioners study.
Approach. Protein Test-Time Training customizes models to one target protein at inference via self-supervised adaptation without additional data.
Contribution. Improves structure prediction on hard targets, achieves state-of-the-art protein fitness, enhances function prediction; improves 19% of Big Fantastic Virus DB.
test-time trainingprotein language modelsself-supervised adaptation
ICLR 2026

OXtal: An All-Atom Diffusion Model for Organic Crystal Structure Prediction

Problem. Predicting 3D organic crystal structures from 2D chemical graphs is computationally expensive and unpredictable, limiting pharmaceutical and semiconductor discovery.
Approach. OXtal is a 100M-parameter diffusion model trained on 600K crystal structures using Stoichiometric Stochastic Shell Sampling (S⁴) for lattice-free, scalable training that directly models conformations and packing.
Contribution. OXtal achieves orders-of-magnitude improvements over prior ML methods while remaining orders cheaper than quantum-chemical approaches for crystal structure prediction.
diffusion modelsall-atom modelingcrystal structure prediction
ICLR 2026

La-Proteina: Atomistic Protein Generation via Partially Latent Flow Matching

Problem. Generating fully atomistic protein structures jointly with amino acid sequences is challenging because side chains vary in length and have complex representations.
Approach. Uses a partially latent protein representation where coarse backbone structure is explicit and sequence/atomistic details are captured via per-residue fixed-dimensionality latent variables, modeled via flow matching.
Contribution. Achieves state-of-the-art on all-atom co-designability, diversity, and structural validity benchmarks, and can generate co-designable proteins up to 800 residues.
flow matchingprotein generationlatent variable modelsstructure design

ICLR 2026

RigidSSL: Rigidity-based Geometric Pretraining for Protein Generation

Problem. Existing protein generation models underutilize vast structural information in large-scale protein datasets; the challenge is how to exploit abundant unlabeled protein data using unsupervised pretraining.
Approach. RigidSSL introduces rigidity-based pretraining using inertial frame canonicalization, combines large-scale perturbations with molecular dynamics views, and applies rigid-body flow matching with Invariant Point Attention.
Contribution. RigidSSL learns stable, geometry-aware representations that improve downstream protein generation by better exploiting low-cost unlabeled protein datasets.
rigidity-based pretrainingflow matchingmolecular dynamics augmentation
ICLR 2026

Constrained Diffusion for Protein Design with Hard Structural Constraints

Problem. Diffusion models offer a powerful means of capturing the manifold of realistic protein structures, enabling rapid design for protein engineering tasks
Approach. However, existing approaches observe critical failure modes when precise constraints are necessary for functional design. To this end, we present a constrained diffusion framework for structure-guided
Contribution. Our approach achieves state-of-the-art, providing perfect satisfaction of bonding and geometric constraints with no degradation in structural diversity.
diffusion
ICLR 2026

PRISM: Enhancing PRotein Inverse Folding through Fine- Grained Retrieval on Structure-Sequence Multimodal Representations

Problem. Inverse protein folding that designs sequences to fold into target structures lacks explicit mechanisms to reuse fine-grained structure-sequence patterns conserved across natural proteins.
Approach. PRISM uses a multimodal retrieval-augmented generation framework that retrieves fine-grained motif representations from known proteins and integrates them with a hybrid self-cross attention decoder.
Contribution. PRISM achieves state-of-the-art perplexity and amino acid recovery on multiple benchmarks while improving foldability metrics (RMSD, TM-score, pLDDT).
retrieval-augmented generationmultimodal learninginverse folding
ICLR 2026

Physically Valid Biomolecular Interaction Modeling with Gauss-Seidel Projection

Problem. Foundation models for biomolecular interaction modeling often produce all-atom structures that violate basic steric feasibility.
Approach. Enforces physical validity as a strict constraint using a differentiable Gauss-Seidel projection that maps provisional coordinates to the nearest physically valid configuration.
Contribution. Achieves ~10× wall-clock speedups compared to 200-step diffusion baselines while guaranteeing physical validity and maintaining structural accuracy.
diffusion modelsprojection methodsbiomolecular structure prediction
ICML 2026

RiboSphere: Learning Unified and Efficient Representations of RNA Structures

Problem. Accurate RNA structure modeling is difficult due to flexible backbones, prevalent non-canonical interactions, and scarcity of experimentally determined 3D structures.
Approach. RiboSphere learns discrete geometric representations by combining SE(3)-invariant geometric transformers with finite scalar quantization and flow-matching decoding.
Contribution. RiboSphere achieves strong reconstruction (RMSD 1.25 Å, TM-score 0.84) and discrete representations transfer effectively to inverse folding and RNA-ligand binding with robust data-scarce performance.
geometric deep learningvector quantizationflow matchingstructure prediction
ICML 2026

Chamaileon: Cross-Context Binder Design with Contextualized Modeling and Mixed Sampling

Problem. Existing protein binder design approaches assume single-target single-state interactions, limiting ability to model multi-target and multi-state requirements.
Approach. Chamaileon uses In-Context Complex Co-Design (I3CD) for context-aware sequence-structure co-modeling and Mixture-of-Paths Sampling optimizing sequences across contexts.
Contribution. Generates sequences adaptable to diverse conformational landscapes and multi-target requirements; benchmarked on newly constructed CROSS dataset.
sequence-structure co-designcontext-aware modelingprotein design
ICML 2026

Protein Design with Agent Rosetta: A Case Study for Specialized Scientific Agents

Problem. ML methods for protein design are restricted to canonical amino acids and narrow objectives, lacking generalist tools for broad design pipelines including non-canonical residues.
Approach. Agent Rosetta pairs LLMs with structured Rosetta environment for iterative design refinement, combining LLM reasoning with physics-based modeling for diverse design objectives.
Contribution. Matches specialized ML models on canonical amino acids and achieves comparable performance on non-canonical residues where ML approaches fail.
protein designllm agentsphysics-based simulationscientific agents
ICML 2026

Towards Sub-Second Molecular Docking as a Structural Primitive: A Quantized Consistency Diffusion Framework

Problem. Molecular docking remains computationally expensive for agent-centered discovery, requiring real-time structural feedback yet remaining limited by iterative refinement and heavy computation.
Approach. Presents compute-efficient co-folding combining Progressive Consistency Regularization for few-step diffusion inference and Residual-Safe Quantization preserving high-fidelity operations.
Contribution. Achieves state-of-the-art docking accuracy with five conformations for 256-token complex in 0.17 seconds on single H20 GPU.
diffusion modelsconsistency regularizationquantization
ICML 2026

Co-Generative De Novo Functional Protein Design

Problem. *De novo* functional protein design aims to generate protein sequences that realize specified biochemical functions without relying on evolutionary templates, enabling broad applications in biotechnology and medicine
Approach. Existing approaches adopt either direct function-to-sequence mapping or decoupled structure-sequence generation strategies but often fail to achieve functionality and foldability simultaneously
Contribution. 2\% in foldability over the strongest baseline
raggenerative models
ICML 2026

TD3B: Transition-Directed Discrete Diffusion for Allosteric Binder Generation

Problem. Structure-based protein binder design optimizes binding to static conformations and cannot represent directional effects or distinguish agonist from antagonist behavior.
Approach. TD3B uses a sequence-based discrete diffusion model with a Direction Oracle for specifying agonist/antagonist behavior, a soft binding-affinity gate, and amortized fine-tuning.
Contribution. TD3B enables targeted agonist and antagonist generation decoupled from binding affinity, unattainable by equilibrium-based or inference-only guidance baselines.
discrete diffusionprotein designdirectional control
ICML 2026

SwitchCraft: A Programmatic Framework for Designing State-Switching Proteins

Problem. Existing protein design frameworks lack ability to design multistate mechanisms that underlie complex natural protein functions.
Approach. Introduce SwitchCraft, a programmatic framework using backpropagation through compositional design constraints parameterized by structure prediction models.
Contribution. Demonstrate in silico success on diverse state-switching primitives and de novo fluorescent biosensor design through compositional constraint optimization.
protein designstructure predictioncompositional constraints
ICML 2026

PDFBench: A Benchmark for De Novo Protein Design from Function

Problem. Function-guided protein design lacks unified comprehensive evaluation framework with inconsistent metrics preventing fair comparison.
Approach. Introduce PDFBench benchmark systematically evaluating eight models on 16 metrics for description-guided and keyword-guided design.
Contribution. Enable reliable model comparisons through comprehensive metric analysis and new SwissTest dataset with strict datetime cutoff.
protein design evaluationbenchmark datasetfunction-guided generation

Building molecules and drugs

13

Using AI to generate molecular structures and small drug-like compounds by understanding chemical space and constraints like synthesizability and binding properties.

ICLR 2026

Refine Drugs, Don’t Complete Them: Uniform-Source Discrete Flows for Fragment-Based Drug Discovery

Problem. Fragment-based drug discovery requires generation methods that can handle de novo and fragment-constrained generation while maintaining property optimization capabilities.
Approach. InVirtuoGen uses discrete flow model for fragmented SMILES shifting paradigm from completion to refinement, combined with genetic algorithm and Proximal Property Optimization for multi-objective lead optimization.
Contribution. Sets state-of-the-art on Practical Molecular Optimization benchmark and yields higher docking scores than baselines, establishing versatile foundation for drug discovery.
discrete flowsmolecular generationfragment-based designproperty optimization

ICLR 2026

SigmaDock: Untwisting Molecular Docking with Fragment-Based SE(3) Diffusion

Problem. Molecular docking via deep learning produces chemically implausible outputs and poor generalization due to complexity of modeling ligand-protein interactions.
Approach. Proposes SigmaDock, an SE(3) Riemannian diffusion model that decomposes ligands into rigid-body fragments and learns to reassemble them within protein binding pockets.
Contribution. Achieves 79.9% Top-1 success rates (RMSD <2 & PB-valid) on PoseBusters, first deep learning approach surpassing classical physics-based docking, with consistent generalization to unseen proteins.
se(3) diffusion modelsfragment-based generationgeometric priorsmolecular docking
★ Read the deep first-principles explainer →
ICLR 2026

Exploring Synthesizable Chemical Space with Iterative Pathway Refinements

Problem. Molecular generative models are not guaranteed to generate synthesizable molecules, and existing solutions struggle to effectively navigate the exponentially large combinatorial space.
Approach. ReaSyn iteratively refines synthetic pathways by: (1) bottom-up and top-down traversal of synthetic trees; (2) discrete flow model edits at pathway level; (3) unified autoregressive model for both directions.
Contribution. ReaSyn achieves highest reconstruction rate, pathway diversity, and optimization performance in synthesizable molecule tasks.
generative modelingmolecular synthesisflow matchingpathway optimization

ICLR 2026

Graph Diffusion Transformers are In-Context Molecular Designers

Problem. Molecular design from scarce labeled data struggles with properties spanning millions of biological assays, and in-context learning shows limited success in this domain.
Approach. DemoDiff uses demonstration-conditioned diffusion with molecule-score examples to guide generation, plus Node Pair Encoding molecular tokenizer requiring 5.5x fewer nodes.
Contribution. DemoDiff matches or surpasses language models 100-1000x larger across 33 design tasks with average rank of 4.10 vs 6.56-17.95 for 19 baselines.
molecular designdiffusion modelsin-context learning
ICLR 2026

FragFM: Hierarchical Framework for Efficient Molecule Generation via Fragment-Level Discrete Flow Matching

Problem. Molecular generation at the atom level is inefficient and difficult to scale for large molecular spaces.
Approach. Introduce FragFM leveraging fragment-level discrete flow matching with coarse-to-fine autoencoders for atom-level reconstruction and stochastic fragment bag strategy for handling extensive fragment spaces.
Contribution. FragFM achieves better property control than atom-based approaches and introduces NPGen benchmark for evaluating natural product-like molecule generation.
fragment-level generationflow matchingdiscrete generation
ICML 2026

MacroGuide: Topological Guidance for Macrocycle Generation

Problem. Macrocycles are underexplored in generative modeling due to scarcity in datasets and challenges of enforcing topological constraints.
Approach. MacroGuide applies diffusion guidance using Persistent Homology to steer pretrained generative models toward macrocycle generation by promoting ring formation at each denoising step.
Contribution. Increases macrocycle generation rates from 1% to 99% while matching or exceeding state-of-the-art quality metrics.
diffusion guidancepersistent homologymolecular generation
ICML 2026

Geometric Pocket-Centric Protein Encoding for Polypharmacology-Guided Multi-Target Drug Design

Problem. Polypharmacology-guided drug design requires satisfying coupled constraints across multiple heterogeneous binding sites, but existing methods model pockets in isolation.
Approach. Proposes pocket-structure-centric generative framework with novel protein representation masking ligand-irrelevant residues while modeling backbone geometry and inter-residue spatial proximity.
Contribution. Generates valid candidates with significantly improved binding affinities on COVID-19, schizophrenia, and tumor targets compared to state-of-the-art methods.
protein representationgenerative modelsmolecular design
ICML 2026

MAST: Motif-Augmented Diffusion with Search Tree for Spectroscopic Molecular Structure Elucidation

Problem. Spectroscopic molecular structure elucidation is challenging due to spectral ambiguity, vast molecular space, and difficulty learning robust spectra-structure relationships from limited paired data.
Approach. MAST introduces explicit motif priors as intermediate evidence throughout diffusion denoising and casts sampling as reward-guided tree search to prioritize high-reward trajectories.
Contribution. Achieves 94.89% exact recovery on QM9S with improved 3D fidelity and high chemical validity while operating under limited computational budgets.
diffusion modelsmolecular generationsearch algorithmsgenerative modeling
ICML 2026

TSMGen: Target-Specific Molecule Generation via Higher-Order Structural Dependencies and Context-Aware Bidirectional Fusion

Problem. Most molecular generation methods for protein targets capture only pairwise amino acid relations and neglect higher-order structural dependencies, limiting molecular quality.
Approach. TSMGen models higher-order spatial dependencies at both atomic and amino acid levels using a context-aware bidirectional fusion module that leverages information from both protein pocket and molecule.
Contribution. Outperforms state-of-the-art in Vina Score, High Affinity, QED, SA and Diversity metrics with case study on β-secretase enzyme confirming stronger binding affinity.
molecular generationgraph neural networksprotein-ligand binding
ICML 2026

CatFlow: Co-generation of Slab-Adsorbate Systems via Flow Matching

Problem. Discovering heterogeneous catalysts tailored for specific reactions remains bottlenecked, as generative models struggle to capture coupling between surface geometry and adsorbate interactions.
Approach. CatFlow, a flow matching framework operating on primitive cell-based factorized slab-adsorbate representation, reducing learnable variables while explicitly encoding surface orientation.
Contribution. Significantly improves structural fidelity of generated catalysts and accurately captures adsorption energy distributions of physically plausible interfaces.
flow matchinggenerative modelsmolecular design
ICML 2026

FlexiFlow: decomposable flow matching for generation of flexible molecular ensemble

Problem. Sampling useful three-dimensional molecular structures along with their most favorable conformations is a key challenge in drug discovery.
Approach. Current state-of-the-art 3D de-novo molecular design generative models are limited to generating a single conformation.
Contribution. However, the conformational landscape of a molecule determines its observable properties and how tightly it is able to bind to a given protein target.
flow matching
ICML 2026

FRIGID: Scaling Diffusion-Based Molecular Generation from Mass Spectra at Training and Inference Time

Problem. De novo molecular structure elucidation from tandem mass spectrometry remains limited by poor scalability during both training and inference.
Approach. FRIGID introduces diffusion language model generating structures via intermediate fingerprints and chemical formulae, with inference-time scaling via forward fragmentation refinement.
Contribution. Achieves 18% Top-1 accuracy on MassSpecGym and triples leading methods on NPLIB1, with log-linear performance scaling at increased compute.
diffusion language modelfingerprint representationfragment refinementmolecular generation
ICML 2026

Order Matters in Retrosynthesis: Structure-aware Generation via Reaction-Center-Guided Discrete Flow Matching

Problem. Template-free retrosynthesis treats the task as black-box sequence generation limiting efficiency, while semi-template methods rely on rigid libraries constraining generalization.
Approach. Structure-aware template-free framework encodes two-stage chemical reactions as positional bias; RetroDiT backbone places reaction center atoms at sequence head with graph transformer and rotary embeddings.
Contribution. Achieves state-of-the-art on USPTO-50k (61.2% top-1) and USPTO-Full (51.3% top-1) with predicted centers; with oracle centers reaches 71.1% and 63.4%, surpassing foundation models with orders of magnitude less data.
retrosynthesisdiscrete flow matchingchemical structure

Simulating physical and biological systems

17

Using AI to model and predict how biological and chemical systems evolve over time, from protein dynamics to weather patterns and neural activity.

ICLR 2026

WFR-FM: Simulation-Free Dynamic Unbalanced Optimal Transport

Problem. Existing Wasserstein-Fisher-Rao (WFR) metric solvers for dynamic unbalanced optimal transport are often unstable, computationally expensive, and difficult to scale.
Approach. WFR Flow Matching (WFR-FM) is a simulation-free training algorithm that unifies flow matching with dynamic unbalanced OT by simultaneously regressing a displacement vector field and a scalar growth rate function for birth-death dynamics.
Contribution. WFR-FM yields more accurate and robust trajectory inference in single-cell biology, reconstructing consistent dynamics with proliferation and apoptosis while outperforming baselines in efficiency and stability.
flow matchingoptimal transportdynamical systems
ICLR 2026

Seeing Through the Brain: New Insights from Decoding Visual Stimuli with fMRI

Problem. Visual stimulus reconstruction from fMRI signals depends critically on identifying the correct latent space structure and generative model that align with neural encoding, but this remains unclear.
Approach. PRISM projects fMRI signals into a structured text space via language model embeddings, using object-centric diffusion to compose individual objects and an attribute-relationship search module to align with neural activity patterns.
Contribution. Demonstrates that text space better captures fMRI signals than vision or joint spaces; object-centric composition reduces detection errors in visual stimulus reconstruction from brain activity.
diffusion modelstext embeddingsobject-centric generationattribute-relationship modeling

ICLR 2026

P3D: Highly Scalable 3D Neural Surrogates for Physics Simulations with Global Context

Problem. High-resolution 3D physics simulations require scalable neural surrogates that capture global context; existing architectures lack speed and accuracy for complex PDE dynamics.
Approach. P3D combines CNN-Transformer backbone for 3D simulations; pretrains on small patches that fuse for global solutions guided by sequence-to-sequence models for long-range dependencies.
Contribution. Outperforms baselines on 14 different PDE dynamics; scales to 512^3 isotropic turbulence; generates probabilistic samples of turbulent flows capturing underlying statistics.
neural surrogatescnn-transformer architecturephysics simulationsdiffusion models

ICLR 2026

SE-Diff: Simulator and Experience Enhanced Diffusion Model for Comprehensive ECG Generation

Problem. Generating realistic ECG signals from clinical context is underexplored, with existing methods overlooking physiological simulators and clinical knowledge.
Approach. SE-Diff integrates an ODE-based ECG simulator into the diffusion process and uses LLM-powered experience retrieval to inject clinical knowledge.
Contribution. SE-Diff improves both signal fidelity and text-ECG semantic alignment over baselines on real-world ECG datasets.
diffusion modelsode simulationknowledge retrieval
ICLR 2026

Align Your Structures: Generating Trajectories with Structure Pretraining for Molecular Dynamics

Problem. Generating molecular dynamics trajectories using deep generative models is challenging due to limited availability of MD data and the complexity of modeling high-dimensional MD distributions.
Approach. The paper proposes a framework that leverages diffusion-based structure generation pre-trained on large-scale conformer data, combined with an interpolator module trained on MD trajectories to enforce temporal consistency.
Contribution. The method excels in generating chemically realistic MD trajectories with remarkable improvements in geometric, dynamical, and energetic measurements on QM9 and DRUGS datasets.
diffusion modelsstructure pre-trainingtemporal consistencymolecular dynamics

ICLR 2026

PROTDYN: A FOUNDATION PROTEIN LANGUAGE MODEL FOR THERMODYNAMICS AND DYNAMICS GENERATION

Problem. Molecular dynamics simulations are computationally expensive, limiting exploration of protein conformational landscapes.
Approach. ProTDyn is a foundation protein language model that unifies conformational ensemble generation and multi-timescale dynamics modeling, enabling flexible i.i.d ensemble sampling and trajectory simulation.
Contribution. Yields thermodynamically consistent ensembles, reproduces dynamical properties across multiple timescales, and generalizes to proteins beyond training data.
protein language modelsmolecular dynamicsthermodynamicsgenerative modeling
ICLR 2026

MarS-FM: Generative Modeling of Molecular Dynamics via Markov State Models

Problem. Molecular dynamics simulations require expensive fine-grained integration over long biomolecular timescales, limiting their applicability.
Approach. MarS-FM learns to sample transitions across discrete Markov State Model states via flow matching, offering two orders of magnitude speedup over MD simulations.
Contribution. Outperforms existing generative MD methods on reproducing structural observables (RMSD, radius of gyration, secondary structure) across diverse protein domains.
markov state modelsflow matchingmolecular dynamicsgenerative modeling
ICML 2026

Marrying Generative Model of Healthcare Events with Digital Twin of Social Determinants of Health for Disease Reasoning

Problem. Existing disease prediction models depend on event-level healthcare representations and lack explicit modeling of social determinants of health (SDoH), limiting personalized disease modeling.
Approach. DiffDT integrates multi-organ sensor data with diffusion models and geometric diffusion for brain networks, conditioned on ICD-coded SDoH proxies for disease trajectory simulation.
Contribution. DiffDT achieves significant improvements over autoregressive baselines on UK Biobank with multi-organ imaging and medical history data for in silico disease reasoning.
diffusion modelsgeometric graph diffusionmulti-organ integration
ICML 2026

Bridging Dynamics and Data: A Unified Diffusion Framework for Mechanistically-Informed Epidemic Forecasting

Problem. Hybrid epidemic forecasting models struggle with data sparsity and non-stationary disease dynamics, often relegating mechanistic priors to auxiliary roles and failing to generalize under distribution shift.
Approach. EpiDiff synergizes epidemiological domain knowledge with diffusion models via an uncertainty-aware steering mechanism that quantifies mechanistic posterior uncertainty and dynamically modulates the diffusion process.
Contribution. Consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in accuracy and robustness particularly under non-stationary distributions, with transparent visualization of when forecasts rely on mechanistic laws versus data-driven patterns.
diffusion modelsmechanistic priorsuncertainty-aware steering
ICML 2026

WIND: Weather Inverse Diffusion for Zero-Shot Atmospheric Modeling

Problem. Weather forecasting and climate modeling require unified approaches that work across diverse atmospheric tasks without task-specific customization.
Approach. WIND is a foundation model pre-trained with self-supervised video reconstruction using unconditional diffusion, then solves diverse atmospheric problems as inverse problems via posterior sampling.
Contribution. Demonstrates a single pre-trained model can replace specialized baselines across forecasting, downscaling, field reconstruction, and extreme weather exploration without fine-tuning.
diffusion modelsinverse problemsvideo reconstructionfoundation models
ICML 2026

ClimateAR: Multi-Scale Autoregressive Generative Modeling for Climate Forecasting

Problem. Deterministic climate models fail to capture uncertainty; existing generative approaches oversimplify by neglecting spatiotemporal dependencies and cross-scale interactions.
Approach. ClimateAR is autoregressive generative model with aligned tokenizer bridging simulation/real-world data and mixed-scale conditioning for multi-scale interactions.
Contribution. Achieves state-of-the-art with 37.56% average anomaly correlation improvement over leading baselines on seasonal-to-interannual forecasting.
autoregressive generationaligned tokenizermixed-scale conditioning
ICML 2026

BioDynaSpec: Harmonic-Guided Spatio-Spectral Autoregressive Diffusion for Protein Dynamics Generation

Problem. Generating long-horizon molecular dynamics is difficult due to error accumulation in time-domain autoregressive models causing drift and fixed step-size constraints on temporal resolution.
Approach. BioDynaSpec reformulates protein dynamics as spatio-spectral generation via Independent Windowed Fourier Decomposition combined with low-to-high frequency autoregression and diffusion denoising.
Contribution. Improves 250-frame trajectory generation with R_250=1.509 Å reducing error by 60.4% versus MDGEN and 57.2% versus ProAR, achieving best PCA and distribution matching metrics.
fourier decompositionspectral generationdiffusion modelsmolecular dynamics
ICML 2026

STFlow: Data-Coupled Flow Matching for Geometric Trajectory Simulation

Problem. Simulating trajectories of N-body systems exhibits high sensitivity to perturbations and multi-scale correlations, challenging existing machine learning approaches.
Approach. STFlow, a generative model based on graph neural networks with hierarchical convolutions, incorporates data-dependent couplings in flow matching to denoise from conditioned random-walks.
Contribution. Achieves lowest prediction errors across N-body systems, molecular dynamics, and human trajectory forecasting with improved scalability and efficiency.
flow matchinggraph neural networkstrajectory generation
ICML 2026

FUSE: FK-Steered Multi-Modal Flow Matching for Efficient Simulation-Based Posterior Estimation

Problem. Simulation-based inference struggles with effective multimodal modeling as existing methods ignore structural disparities between parameters and observations via brute-force fusion strategies.
Approach. FUSE employs a dual-track architecture preserving distinct features of multimodal inputs with FK-steered sampling leveraging intermediate observation likelihoods to guide generative trajectories.
Contribution. Outperforms state-of-the-art on SBI benchmarks and successfully resolves complex parameter degeneracies in exoplanet orbital estimation.
feynman-kac steeringflow matchingsimulation-based inference
ICML 2026

LoPhyDA: Low-Rank Tensor and Physics Gradient Guided Diffusion for Atmospheric Data Assimilation

Problem. Data assimilation for atmospheric prediction remains constrained by high dimensionality, reliance on imperfect state-observation mappings, and error accumulation in observation-sparse regions.
Approach. LoPhyDA is a diffusion assimilation algorithm dual-guided by low-rank tensor completion exploiting spatial continuity and physical constraints via PDE residual gradients to rectify the generative trajectory.
Contribution. LoPhyDA outperforms state-of-the-art generative assimilation models in global weather prediction, achieving robust and physically consistent assimilation.
data assimilationtensor completiondiffusion modelspde constraints
ICML 2026

PathwayLLM: Explainable Clinical Trajectory Modeling with Structured Pathways for Sepsis Prediction

Problem. Sepsis prediction requires models integrating heterogeneous structured evidence from EHRs while tracking clinical deterioration over time.
Approach. PathwayLLM grounds prediction on temporal signals, graph-structured evidence, and pathway-level signals, encoding observations from multiple views injected into pretrained language models.
Contribution. On MIMIC-IV, the method achieves AUROC 0.891 and AUPRC 0.724, outperforming baselines with clinically relevant explanations.
trajectory modelinggraph encodingclinical prediction
ICML 2026

A Regime-Aware Trajectory Prediction Framework for 1000+ Systems Biology Models

Problem. Predicting long-horizon trajectories of biological dynamical systems remains challenging due to substantial system heterogeneity, with most approaches being system-specific.
Approach. Creates benchmark of over 1,000 ODE-based systems biology models and proposes regime-aware framework introducing structured initial states derived from biological regime priors into conditional flow matching.
Contribution. Achieves state-of-the-art accuracy (31% MAE reduction), well-calibrated uncertainty (17% CRPS improvement), and efficient long-horizon inference across benchmark.
trajectory predictionconditional flow matchingsystems biologyuncertainty quantification

Directing flow models and diffusion to build specific structures

39

Using flow matching and diffusion models with physics constraints and conditioning to guide the generation toward desired properties and realistic outputs.

ICLR 2026

Towards a Certificate of Trust: Task-Aware OOD Detection for Scientific AI

Problem. Data-driven scientific models fail on out-of-distribution data but detecting failures in regression tasks lacks practical solutions, threatening deployment in critical fields.
Approach. Proposes OOD detection via score-based diffusion models estimating joint likelihood of inputs and predictions, providing task-aware reliability scores.
Contribution. Shows likelihood strongly correlates with prediction error across scientific datasets including PDEs and satellite imagery, providing step toward verifiable certificate of trust.
ood detectiondiffusion modelslikelihood estimationregression reliability

ICLR 2026

FlowCast: Advancing Precipitation Nowcasting with Conditional Flow Matching

Problem. Radar-based precipitation nowcasting requires modeling high-dimensional spatiotemporal data under atmospheric uncertainty while maintaining computational efficiency for time-critical applications.
Approach. FlowCast leverages Conditional Flow Matching for direct noise-to-data mapping in compressed latent space, replacing iterative sampling of diffusion models with rapid sample generation.
Contribution. FlowCast establishes new state-of-the-art in probabilistic performance with significantly greater efficiency than diffusion objectives while maintaining high performance with fewer sampling steps.
conditional flow matchinglatent space generationspatiotemporal forecasting
ICLR 2026

Learning Escorted Protocols For Multistate Free-Energy Estimation

Problem. Estimating relative free energy differences between multiple thermodynamic states requires learning effective switching protocols that balance deterministic and stochastic steps.
Approach. The method uses Conditional Flow Matching to learn escorted protocols between thermodynamic states and introduces Conditional Density Matching for free-energy estimation, further reducing variance via a Flow Graph that couples multiple flows.
Contribution. The framework enables learning of efficient switching protocols for multistate free-energy estimation with improved consistency across different transition paths.
conditional flow matchingconditional density matchingflow graphthermodynamic sampling

ICLR 2026

Functional MRI Time Series Generation via Wavelet-Based Image Transform and Spectral Flow Matching for Brain Disorder Identification

Problem. Synthesizing realistic fMRI data that preserves non-stationarity, spatiotemporal dynamics, and physiological variations of BOLD signals remains challenging for generative models.
Approach. DSFM cascades dual frequency representations (wavelet and cosine transforms) with spectral flow matching: DWT captures transient multi-scale variations, DCT captures low-frequency energy, then flow matching generates class-conditioned cosine representations.
Contribution. The framework generates physiologically plausible fMRI time-domain BOLD signals with improved downstream brain network classification.
wavelet transformdiscrete cosine transformspectral flow matchingclass-conditioned generation
★ Read the deep first-principles explainer →
ICLR 2026

FALCON: Few-step Accurate Likelihoods for Continuous Flows

Problem. Continuous normalizing flows for molecular Boltzmann sampling have exact likelihood computation but likelihood calculation is extremely costly, requiring thousands of function evaluations per sample.
Approach. FALCON introduces a hybrid training objective that encourages invertibility to allow few-step sampling with accurate likelihood suitable for importance sampling applications.
Contribution. FALCON outperforms state-of-the-art normalizing flow models for molecular Boltzmann sampling and is two orders of magnitude faster than equivalently performing CNF models.
normalizing flowsflow matchingmolecular sampling

Problem. Computational protein design requires substantial domain expertise with traditional physics-based methods or relies on restricted functional ontologies that fail to bridge semantic gap between text and protein sequences.

Problem. Spoken Language Models in low-resource languages rely on synthetic data which improves phonetic accuracy but progressively suppresses prosodic variability, causing expressivity collapse (Synthetic Erosion).
Approach. Two self-alignment frameworks address this: Disentanglement-Guided Self-Alignment recovers expressivity via prosody-timbre separation, and Temperature-Driven Self-Critique stabilizes generation through automated exploration and filtering.

Identifying Connectivity Distributions from Neural Dynamics Using Flows

Training neural networks to predict system behavior instead of running expensive simulations, so scientists can explore what-if scenarios quickly.

KANO: Kolmogorov-Arnold Neural Operator

Problem. Fourier Neural Operators (FNO) fail on position-dependent dynamics and have spectral decay assumptions limiting practical applicability to many PDEs.
Approach. Introduces KANO using dual-domain parameterization with spectral and spatial bases, including symbolic interpretability that overcomes FNO spectral bottleneck.
Contribution. Robustly generalizes on position-dependent operators where FNO fails, reconstructs quantum Hamiltonians in closed-form symbolic representations with ~6e-6 state infidelity, vastly outperforming FNO.

ComPhy: Composing Physical Models with end-to-end Alignment

Problem. Solving systems of partial differential equations accurately remains a challenge because methods typically treat all equations uniformly without leveraging the physical structure of the problem.
Approach. ComPhy assigns each PDE to a dedicated learning module capable of incorporating state-of-the-art techniques like Physics-Informed Neural Networks, and introduces an end-to-end alignment mechanism for knowledge transfer between modules.
Contribution. ComPhy is the first approach specifically designed for systems of PDEs and outperforms single-model approaches on multiple benchmark problems.

Harmonized Cone for Feasible and Non-conflict Directions in Training Physics-Informed Neural Networks

Problem. Training Physics-Informed Neural Networks is difficult due to multi-objective loss coupling PDE residuals, boundary conditions, and physics terms, yielding infeasible or conflicting updates.
Approach. HARMONIC defines the harmonized cone as the intersection of primal and dual cones of per-loss gradients, computing updates within this feasible non-conflict region via the Double Description method.
Contribution. A training procedure with convergence guarantees in nonconvex settings that generally outperforms state-of-the-art PDE solvers while ensuring feasible and non-conflicting updates.

Overcoming PINNs Failure Modes In High Dimension With Low-Rank Fourier Sum

Problem. Physics-informed neural networks fail on oscillatory, multiscale, stiff, or long-time solutions; high-dimensional collocation-based training yields large numerical errors and high-variance gradients.
Approach. Low-Rank Fourier Sums (LoRFS) represent solutions as low-rank separable Fourier expansions enabling closed-form evaluation of physics objectives and their gradients.
Contribution. Eliminates sampling noise and consistently outperforms PINN baselines across failure modes while remaining stable in high-dimensional regimes.
physics-informed neural networksfourier expansionsdifferential equationsnumerical methods

Problem. Physiological time series are highly nonstationary with localized transient events, challenging both PDE-oriented neural operators and conventional deep models.
Approach. NeurOCNN integrates continuous-time spline-parameterized convolutions for localized morphology with Fourier projection pooling for discretization-invariant inference.
Contribution. Outperforms standard neural-operator baselines and achieves performance comparable to state-of-the-art with stable accuracy across multiple unseen sampling rates.

Approach. Searches over compositions of training operators at test time to approximate unseen dynamics, building on DISCO which provides a dictionary of operators trained across different physics.

Expanding the Chaos: Neural Operator for Stochastic (Partial) Differential Equations

Problem. Learning solution operators for stochastic differential equations and stochastic partial differential equations is important but remains challenging for scaling.
Approach. Builds on Wiener-chaos expansions to design neural operator architectures; projects noise paths onto orthonormal Wick-Hermite features and uses neural operator backbones to parameterize chaos coefficients.
Contribution. Achieves competitive accuracy across SPDE benchmarks and SDE-based applications including diffusion image sampling, graph interpolation, and financial extrapolation; effective even when Brownian trajectories are unobserved.

ICML 2026

G-RANS: Generalizable Residual-Aware Neural Solvers for Sparse Systems

Problem. Neural operators and hybrid neural iterative schemes struggle with sparse linear systems from PDE discretization due to poor generalization across parameters and stagnation as residuals evolve.
Approach. G-RANS performs residual-aware subspace corrections by mapping residuals to matrix graphs, generating multi-scale correction subspaces via a basis generator, and applying projected updates through differentiable projection; trained self-supervised via progressive bootstrap.
Contribution. Achieves 10^-5 order residuals, substantially outperforming end-to-end neural operators, with 2-4× less wall-clock time than Krylov solvers, while showing robustness to ±70% coefficient shifts.
neural operatorsresidual-aware learningsubspace methods
ICML 2026

Heavy-tailed Physics-Informed Neural Networks

Problem. PINNs training with MSE loss assumes independent Gaussian residuals, but actual residuals are heterogeneous and heavy-tailed, causing large residuals to dominate optimization.
Approach. Adopts Student-t residual model with equivalent hierarchical EM algorithm alternating between weight estimation and weighted MSE optimization.
Contribution. Bounding influence of extreme residuals and admits convergence guarantees under standard assumptions; demonstrates improved solution accuracy and robustness on diverse PDE benchmarks.
robust loss functionsexpectation-maximizationphysics-informed learning
ICML 2026

Taming the Loss Landscape of PINNs with Noisy Feynman–Kac Supervision: Operator Preconditioning and Non-Asymptotic Error Bounds

Problem. PINNs train slowly or fail on challenging PDEs due to severely ill-conditioned loss landscapes inherited from the underlying differential operator.
Approach. Study PINNs augmented with pointwise data-fidelity supervision; propose FK-PINNs using Feynman-Kac functional Monte Carlo labels and show operator-level preconditioning effect.
Contribution. Derive non-asymptotic L²(Ω)-error bounds for FK-PINNs; establish pseudo-dimension bounds for tanh network derivatives; demonstrate successful solutions on PDEs where standard PINNs fail.

Adding physics rules to neural learning

31

Forcing neural networks to respect the laws of physics (like energy conservation or differential equations) during training, so they stay realistic even on new data.

(U)NFV: Supervised and Unsupervised Neural Finite Volume Methods for Solving Hyperbolic PDEs

Approach. (U)NFV generalizes classical FV methods by learning update rules over extended stencils while preserving conservation, supporting both supervised and unsupervised (weak-form residual) training.
Contribution. Achieves up to 10x lower error than Godunov's method, outperforms ENO/WENO, and rivals discontinuous Galerkin solvers on conservation laws and traffic modeling.

InputDSA: Demixing, then comparing recurrent and externally driven dynamics

Problem. Dynamical Similarity Analysis (DSA) ignores how inputs drive dynamics, misclassifying similar systems driven differently—a critical issue for real-world non-autonomous systems.
Approach. Introduces InputDSA extending DSA by estimating and comparing both input and intrinsic dynamic operators using Dynamic Mode Decomposition with control (DMDc) based on subspace identification.
Contribution. Successfully compares partially observed input-driven systems from noisy data and identifies that high-performing RNNs are dynamically similar while low-performing ones are more diverse.

A Cartesian-3j Framework for Machine Learning Interatomic Potentials

Problem. Machine learning interatomic potentials (MLIPs) are typically built with spherical tensors, but Cartesian tensor formulations are more naturally aligned with atomic coordinates and targets, yet remain underdeveloped.
Approach. Develop a Cartesian framework for irreducible Cartesian tensors (ICTs) by introducing Cartesian-3j symbols and Cartesian Generalized Clebsch-Gordan Coefficients as analogues of spherical tensor coupling, enabling controlled comparison of tensor bases.

FlashSchNet redesigns SchNet-style GNN-MD with four IO-aware techniques: flash radial basis fusing distance computation and basis expansion, flash message passing eliminating edge tensor materialization, flash aggregation via CSR segment reduce, and channel-wise 16-bit quantization of MLP weights.

SGERA: Stein-Guided ECG-Report Alignment for ECG Representation Learning

Problem. Electrocardiogram (ECG) representation learning via ECG-report alignment is often hindered by the inherent structural and statistical divergence between signals and natural language.
Approach. Existing methods struggle to bridge this gap with simple contrastive objectives, but struggle with distribution dependencies between heterogeneous features.
Contribution. To address this, we propose **SGERA** (**S**tein-**G**uided **E**CG-**R**eport **A**lignment), which leverages the unique properties of Stein kernels to provide a more rigorous geometric alignment in the latent space: **instance-level** alignment via a Stein-RBF kernel enforces pairwise consistency between ECG and report embeddings and **distribution-level** alignment via a Stein-Score kernel captures higher-order interactions for global alignment.
contrastive learning

Contribution. Achieves 20–54% reductions in rollout CRPS and up to 30% improvements in variance-normalized RMSE on single-system models, and 40% improvement in CRPS on PDE foundation models, with modest additional training cost.

An Information-Theoretical Framework For Optimizing Experimental Design To Distinguish Probabilistic Neural Codes

Approach. An information-theoretical framework optimizes task stimulus distributions to maximize the information gap, defined as the expected performance difference when likelihood versus posterior decoders are applied to neural populations, by evaluating KL divergence between true and task-marginalized surrogate posteriors.
Contribution. The information gap accurately predicts decoder performance differences across diverse task settings, and maximizing it yields stimulus distributions that optimally differentiate competing probabilistic coding hypotheses.

Approach. Analyzes scaling laws for weather models finding that data scaling shows stronger effects than parameter scaling and optimal allocation favors larger training datasets over increased model size.
Contribution. Aurora exhibits strongest data-scaling (10x data→3.2x loss reduction) and reveals weather models favor increased width over depth, contrasting language model scaling behaviors.
scaling lawsweather forecastingdata efficiency

Contribution. Achieves 90.81-93.15% solution quality, 29.7-31.1% improvement over SOTA, and 83.3% convergence speedup with robust performance across domains and LLM backbones.

Contribution. CLEF improves immediate sequence editing accuracy by 16.28% MAE on average and delayed sequence editing by 26.73% MAE, and achieves 63.19% MAE improvement on counterfactual trajectory generation under zero-shot settings.
temporal concept learningconditional generationcounterfactual inference

Contribution. Achieves up to 20x lower error than prior neural PDE methods and reduces wall-clock convergence time by 2-10x with per-epoch times as low as 3.5ms for 17M parameter models.
neural hash encodinghermite interpolationmultigrid curriculum
